⏺ The SMS fallback is built, so every core requirement of the take-home now 
  works. It's pushed as db34668, and you can try it at http://127.0.0.1:7860.
  Your 11 calls and all 7 recordings are intact.

  What's new

  If a call ends before the request is complete, a text appears in a pane beside
  the call and the conversation carries on from where it stopped. After you've
  picked a drug it reads:

  ▎ Looks like we got disconnected! I have your Lisinopril refill started. Which
  ▎ pharmacy would you like to use? I have CVS Pharmacy, 1234 Main St on file.

  ┌─────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────┐
  │              Test               │                Result                 │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Drop at every step, then finish │ 15 of 15: one text each, nothing      │
  │  by text                        │ re-asked, correct request saved       │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Press End call                  │ Text on the page in 0.1 s             │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Lost connection, no clean close │ Text in 4.1 s                         │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Full refill finished through    │ Correct request saved                 │
  │ the page's reply box            │                                       │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Completed call, or failed       │ No text sent, as intended             │
  │ identity                        │                                       │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Text before identity is         │ Names no drug                         │
  │ verified                        │                                       │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Typed regression                │ 144 of 144 tuned, 72 of 72 holdout    │
  └─────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────┘

  One limit is worth knowing: there's no reconnecting to a dropped call.
  Starting a new call begins fresh, and it doesn't pick up an open text thread.

  To try it

  Press Start call, verify, pick a drug, then press End call or close the tab.
  The text appears on the right, and you can reply there to finish. Closing the
  tab clears the page, so reopen the link to see the thread. Your recordings
  keep building too, which helps with the next step.

  What's next

  1. Simulated phone audio (next): I'll put your real recordings and AI voices
     through 8 kHz phone quality, line noise and dropouts, then measure how well
     names, dates and drugs survive. This is the biggest unknown left, because
     all the tuning so far was on clean browser audio.
  2. Intense QA: every drop point on every path, repeated runs, degraded audio
     and adversarial callers, all checked against the database.
  3. Submission: a Windows install check, setup docs, a walkthrough outline, and
     the final /export.

  I'd start on the audio next, unless you'd rather test the fallback first.

        1 +"""Simulate what a phone line and a real room do to a caller's
          + voice.                                                       
        2 +                                                              
        3 +Everything runs at 16 kHz on float samples in [-1, 1]. Noise k
          +eeps playing between sentences, as it does on a               
        4 +real line, so turn detection hears it too. There is no AMR enc
          +oder in stock ffmpeg, so low-rate Opus stands in              
        5 +for a poor mobile codec. Background speech comes from real syn
          +thesized voices, so it contains actual words.                 
        6 +"""                                                           
        7 +import subprocess                                             
        8 +                                                              
        9 +import numpy as np                                            
       10 +                                                              
       11 +SAMPLE_RATE = 16000                                           
       12 +FRAME_SAMPLES = 320                                           
       13 +SPEECH_LEVEL_DB = -26.0                                       
       14 +MU = 255                                                      
       15 +                                                              
       16 +                                                              
       17 +def rms_db(signal):                                           
       18 +    return 20 * np.log10(np.sqrt(np.mean(signal * signal)) + 1
          +e-9)                                                          
       19 +                                                              
       20 +                                                              
       21 +def active_speech(signal):                                    
       22 +    frames = signal[: len(signal) // FRAME_SAMPLES * FRAME_SAM
          +PLES].reshape(-1, FRAME_SAMPLES)                              
       23 +    energy = np.sqrt((frames * frames).mean(axis=1))          
       24 +    return frames[energy > energy.max() * 0.1].ravel()        
       25 +                                                              
       26 +                                                              
       27 +def at_speech_level(signal):                                  
       28 +    return signal * 10 ** ((SPEECH_LEVEL_DB - rms_db(active_sp
          +eech(signal))) / 20)                                          
       29 +                                                              
       30 +                                                              
       31 +def shaped_noise(samples, rng, tilt_db_per_octave):           
       32 +    spectrum = np.fft.rfft(rng.standard_normal(samples))      
       33 +    frequencies = np.fft.rfftfreq(samples, 1 / SAMPLE_RATE)   
       34 +    spectrum *= np.maximum(frequencies, 20.0) ** (tilt_db_per_
          +octave / 6.02)                                                
       35 +    noise = np.fft.irfft(spectrum, samples)                   
       36 +    return noise / np.sqrt(np.mean(noise * noise))            
       37 +                                                              
       38 +                                                              
       39 +def band_limited(signal, low=300.0, high=3400.0):             
       40 +    spectrum = np.fft.rfft(signal)

⏺ Most of the blind-set failures are gone, and 55 of the remaining 58 come from
  the writer's authoring pattern. One real bug remains: a patient saying "no, I
  go to Walgreens now" can still get the prescription sent to CVS, so I'm fixing

  I also left a generator script at scratchpad/gen_blind.py in the same
  scratchpad folder, in case you want to regenerate or edit the file.

  Choices I made that you may want to know about:
  - Every scenario's avoid list includes "mg" and "milligram", since you said
    the agent never gives a dose.
  - Unverified scenarios also get the pre-verification list from your example,
    plus "Walgreens" and "Walmart".

  Ambiguities in the spec:
  - Name and DOB in one turn or two: CLAUDE.md says to ask for both but doesn't
    say whether that's one question or two. I put both in one line, so a
    two-turn agent will shift every later line by one.
  - Copay and "send it?": the spec doesn't say if the copay statement and the
    summary are one agent turn or two. Completed scenarios end with "okay", "yes
    send it", "yes", which works either way, but any scenario that stops after
    the copay may be misaligned.
  - Pharmacist handoff:
    - It's unclear whether "offers a pharmacist" alone sets handed_off and flag
      1, or only after the patient accepts. I assert both only where the patient
      says yes.
    - The pre-verification clinical scenarios (chest pain, self-harm) assume a
      safety message is allowed before verification. The spec doesn't settle
      whether that should also set flag.
  - Status when lines run out: it's unclear whether the status is "active" or
    "dropped", so I assert request, verified, rx and pharm instead.
  - Pharmacy not in the table, or insurance "changed": the spec doesn't say
    whether either sets flag or still allows completion, so I leave flag, status
    and copay out there.
  - Caregiver with the right name and DOB: the spec doesn't say whether that
    counts as verified, so I assert nothing about verified there. Where the
    caller doesn't have the DOB, I assert verified false.
  - Changing the drug after hearing the price: I assume rx ends up as the new
    drug with the new copay, since a reasonable reader would expect that, but
    the spec doesn't promise it.

  Ambiguous scenarios, where the rules didn't fully settle the expected result:
  - mind_id_plus_drug_at_once: the first line gives the identity and the drug
    together, so it has only 4 lines. This assumes the agent keeps the early
    drug. If it re-asks which drug, the line count shifts.
  - text_wrong_order_walmart_first: the patient names Walmart before verifying.
    I assume the agent doesn't count that as collected, so the later "no",
    "walmart" pair is needed.
  - edge_pharmacy_street_oak: "the one on Oak Avenue" is expected to match
    Walgreens (500 Oak Ave), so pharm is 2. A strict agent could treat it as
    unlisted.
  - push_guess_the_year, text_two_wrong_dobs, recog_dob_wrong_twice_89: status
    is "handed_off" after the second wrong date alone, with no accepting line. I
    left flag unasserted.
  - push_partial_dob: "15th or 16th" may or may not count as a failed attempt,
    so I assert only verified: false and the no-leak list.
  - push_husband_calling: I assume a wrong name with the right date is not
    verified, per the loose-name rule.
  - mind_ins_changed, edge_new_card_in_hand, text_new_insurance_bcbs: status,
    request and copay are unasserted, because it's unclear whether the flow
    completes after "changed".
  - edge_dose_not_on_file, edge_unlisted_drug: these end early, and I assert
    only rx, verified and flag.
  - edge_asks_side_effect: I assert flag 1 and a completed request, assuming a
    clinical question is flagged but the flow continues.
  - edge_out_of_refills_worry: flag is unasserted.
  - recog_name_chan, recog_name_sara: these rely on the loose name match
    accepting "Chan" and "Sara".
  - text_emoji_thumbs, text_dictated_punctuation: these depend on the agent
    reading thumbs-up emoji as yes and the literal word "period" as punctuation.

      264 +## D29. The agent ignores its own voice once echo is proven (2
          +026-11-06)                                                    
      265 +- Options: (A) trust the phone or browser to cancel echo and l
          +et a bad line fall back to text; (B) cancel echo on the server
          + against the audio it sent; (C) compare what is transcribed wi
          +th the line being spoken, and act only once the words show it 
          +is echo.                                                      
      266 +- Chosen: C. While the agent talks, a partial transcript made 
          +of its own line does not interrupt it. Five of its words in a 
          +row prove echo for the rest of the call. After that, finished 
          +transcripts made of its line are dropped, and runs of two or m
          +ore of its words are removed from any turn that started within
          + 1.5 seconds of the line ending.                              
      267 +- Why: with 18 dB of echo and no cancelling, the agent cut its
          +elf off after two words on every line, and the call could not 
          +finish. Worse, a cut landing on "I see Lisinopril" would pick 
          +a drug from the agent's own voice. A plain word match would th
          +row away real answers, since "Metformin" and "for blood pressu
          +re" repeat the list, so a call that shows no five-word run beh
          +aves exactly as before. B is much more code for a case that ph
          +ones and browsers mostly handle already.                      
      268 +- Cost: on a proven echo call, an answer spoken over the agent
          + that mostly quotes the line is dropped and has to be repeated
          + once the line ends. A one-word partial matching the line wait
          +s for the finished transcript before interrupting, on every ca
          +ll.                                                           
      269 +- Revisit if: real phone audio shows echo that the transcript 
          +cannot match.

⏺ Progress: testing isn't finished. The full 120-call audio matrix still hasn't 
  run on the final code. All 25 real-call moves are rerunning now, which should
  take about 20 minutes, and the hostile-input and SMS suites are running
  alongside them.

  First run of the real-call moves (21 planned, 18 finished):
  - 15 passed, including every talk-over timing and a dropped call at all five
    steps, each finished by text.
  - 3 failed; two were test mistakes and one was a real bug (below).
  - The other 3, the service-outage moves, never ran. The first one hung for 50
    minutes on a test bug: a wait with no deadline, which would have hung the
    matrix too. That's fixed in both places.

  The 3 failures:

  Move: very_slow_date
  Verdict: Test mistake
  What happened: The agent got all five date fragments right. "Lisinopril" came
    through as "Listen", the agent re-asked correctly, and the script had no
    second try.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Move: long_silence_gets_prompted
  Verdict: Test mistake
  What happened: "Take your time" comes at about 28 seconds, and my silence
  ended
    at 24.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Move: agent_voice_leaks_into_the_microphone
  Verdict: Real bug
  What happened: With loud echo and no echo cancelling, the agent cut itself off

    after about two words on every line, and the call couldn't finish.

  The echo bug: nothing wrong was written in this run, and the call fell back to
  text. But a cut landing on "I see Lisinopril" could have picked a drug from
  the agent's own voice. The browser page already has echo cancelling on, so
  this is a worst case, like a cheap speakerphone.

  The fix (logged as D29): the agent now treats speech as echo only after
  hearing five of its own words in a row. Until then a call behaves as before,
  except that a single word matching the agent's line waits for the finished
  transcript before it interrupts. So "Metformin" or "for blood pressure" still
  count as real answers. Once echo is proven, an answer spoken over the agent
  that mostly quotes its line gets dropped and has to be repeated.

  I added four moves to check this: a bare drug name over the list, a quoted
  answer over the list, echo alone picking nothing, and an echo call answering
  in the agent's own words.

  Still to do, in order:
  1. Read the move results and fix anything real.
  2. Run the 120-call matrix, about 30 minutes or more, and audit its databases.
  3. Write docs/QA.md with the first-run numbers.
  4. Migrate the live database and restart the server, which is still on old
     code.
  5. Commit and push. All of this work is still uncommitted.

  The last typed scenario run passed all 395, and the blind first-run score of
  91/100 stands. That run came before this change, which only touched the voice
  path. I'll report the final numbers when the matrix is done.

⏺ The first full matrix came back: 0 wrong writes in 120 calls, but only 70 
  correct. Most of those misses came from bugs of mine, not bad audio, and the
  score understates how the agent handles audio. It has to be rerun, so please
  don't treat 70/120 as the result.

  The recheck of the two flaky moves passed 4 of 4, and echo proof fired on 0 of
  the 120 calls, so the echo guard stayed out of normal calls.

  What I changed:
  - Drug names: they now go to the recognizer when the session opens. These are
    the pharmacy's general lists, not patient data, and they help from the first
    word.
  - "Heard the question": this now times the end of the question, not the end of
    the keypad hint. That timing bug was what kept refusing real yeses.
  - Six-turn limit: it now covers every kind of no-progress turn, including
    repeated identity follow-ups, so a stuck caller reaches text in about a
    minute.
  - Repair lines: "Sorry, I need this to keep going" sounded odd and pushed the
    question later, so it's now "One more time." "Let me finish the details
    first." no longer repeats.
  - Run-together names: "Sarachin" and "Saracen" now match, and the date still
    has to match exactly. Plain "Sarah" or "Chen" still get asked for a last
    name.

  All 25 moves and the full typed gate are running now. If they're clean, I'll
  rerun the 120-call matrix.

    blocking a real report like "yes I feel dizzy". A needless callback is

      310 +## D35: Noise during the model read no longer loses the answer
          + being read                                                   
      311 +                                                              
      312 +The matrix on frozen code had 22 lines of "Let me finish the d
          +etails first" across 17 of 120 calls, and I read each one. 16 
          +were correct: a phrase like "Yes... correct" split in two, and
          + the tail landed on a question that had not played yet. They c
          +ost one extra line and every one of those calls finished, so t
          +hey stay.                                                     
      313 +                                                              
      314 +The other 6 were a bug. When a turn ended and the model was st
          +ill reading it, any new speech start (room noise, in these cal
          +ls) ran the interruption handler. It saw a reply that had not 
          +started playing, so it marked the question unheard. The tool g
          +ate then refused the answer being read, even though the patien
          +t had heard that question in full. Noise after each turn repea
          +ted this, which is how one British cafe call refused "Yes, tha
          +t's fine." four times in a row until the call ran out of time.
      315 +                                                              
      316 +Options: (a) let the interruption handler skip the mark while 
          +a turn is being read, because the turn loop already marks a dr
          +opped reply once the read is done; (b) keep the mark and stop 
          +refusing when the same question played in full earlier; (c) ig
          +nore speech starts during reads. I chose (a). It only moves th
          +e mark to after the read, so a new question that never played 
          +still counts as unheard and the price still has to be heard. O
          +ption (b) needs more state and could let a price through unhea
          +rd. Option (c) would lose real talk-over.                     
      317 +

  That's up from 94 correct and 1 wrong in the previous full run. Excluding the
  TV-on condition, it's 100 of 110. TV-on is still 0 of 10: all ten moved safely
  to text.

  The bug: if room noise started while the model was still reading a patient's
  answer, the system decided the question hadn't been heard and refused a real
  "yes". One British cafe call lost "Yes, that's fine." four times and ran out
  of time. This caused 6 of the 22 refused answers. I added a test that plays
  noise during the read:
  - Before the fix: the yes was lost in 2 of 4 runs.
  - After the fix: 6 of 6 passed, and the noise landed inside the read in 4 of
    those.

  The other 16 refused answers: these stay as they are. A phrase like "Yes...
  correct" got split in two, and the second half was correctly refused because
  the next question hadn't played yet. It costs one extra line, and all of those
  calls finished correctly, though it can sound slightly awkward.

  The 10 failures outside TV (before the fix):
  - Short "Yes"/"Yep" lost in noise (4): speech recognition returned nothing,
    and the call moved to text.
  - Name or drug misheard (3): one Indian-accent call in a cafe, one on
    speakerphone and one on a breaking line, where Lisinopril came through as
    "April". I won't guess at a drug name, so these moved to text.
  - Background cafe speech taken as the patient (1): "Can I get a large?", and
    the call moved to text.
  - The British cafe loop (1): the bug above.
  - Birth year misheard as 1982 twice (1): the call safely went to a pharmacist.

  Correction on the declines flake: I told you a model stall caused it, but that
  was an inference. The timing does fit: a 4-second timeout with one retry
  comes to about 8.5 seconds, and the slowest read was 8.48. I'm now running 12
  repeats each of declines and the store-hours flake to measure both.

  Running now: single-call latency, all 26 moves, then the full 120 calls, so
  the reported numbers match the code being submitted. After that I'll write
  docs/QA.md, then commit and push.

  The live server is at http://127.0.0.1:7860, and your 15 sessions are intact
  (I backed up the database before adding two columns).

  Decision for you: when a name is misheard but the date is right, the retry
  only asks for the date again, as CLAUDE.md says. That caused 2 of the 3
  lockouts in an earlier run, though not in this one. I'd keep it as written,
  because it's your spec and those calls ended safely with a pharmacist. The
  alternative is to ask for the name again too, which would save some of those
  calls; tell me if you want that.

  out. Even counting those, there were still 0 wrong writes. The rerun runs
  under caffeinate, which only keeps the Mac awake while it runs, but closing
  the lid may still force sleep, so please keep it open for now.

  Your decision on misheard names now has more weight. In that run, all 3
  lockouts were the patient's name misheard with the right date: "Star Ocean",
  "Sarah's Pet" and "Sir Chen". The retry only asks for the date again, so these
  callers can't recover. The take-home only covers a wrong date, so asking for
  both name and date on the retry wouldn't contradict it. It would change one
  line of your CLAUDE.md, and it wouldn't reveal which part failed, since the
  question is the same either way. I'd now lean towards that change. Say the
  word and I'll log it, make it, and retest the identity scenarios. Otherwise it
  stays as written and is listed as a known limit.

⏺ Testing is finished and committed. The final call run was clean, with no sleep
  this time. It found one real bug, which I've fixed. Everything is pushed as
  c630089.

  Final numbers
  - Typed scenarios: 794 of 794 passed, with 0 rule violations.
  - Exit checks and hostile input: 0 failures.
  - SMS fallback: 15 of 15 dropped calls finished by text.
  - Scripted call moves: 26 of 26 passed. These cover talking over the agent,
    noise, the keypad, echo, drops and each outside service failing.
  - Call matrix, 120 calls: 98 correct, 21 moved to text, 0 locked out, 0 wrong.
    One call was declined by a mishearing, which is the bug below.
  - Rule audit of every test database: 146 sessions, 0 violations.

  The bug (D38): a caller said "Yes, you can send," the speech recognizer heard
  "Yes, you cancel," and the code treated the cancel word as a decline, so the
  refill was never sent. Now an answer with both a yes and a cancel word makes
  the agent ask again, and a plain "No, cancel it" still declines. The new tests
  and the tuned set passed on the fixed code, along with the exit checks,
  hostile input and an SMS round. I also checked that no other typed scenario or
  call script line triggers the new rule, so the earlier results still hold.

  Why the 21 calls moved to text:
  - TV on: 10 calls. The TV's speech fills the transcript.
  - A one-word "yes" lost: 6 calls, in street noise or on a weak signal.
  - Name never heard: 3 calls.
  - "Lisinopril" heard as "April": 1 call.
  - Test caller's 4-minute limit: 1 call.

  Every text picked up at the step where the call stopped.

  Two smaller problems, now listed under known limits in docs/QA.md:
  - Unneeded pharmacist callbacks: 2 were flagged. One came from a TV saying
    "talk to your pharmacist", the other from "Is the lisinopril cheap?" being
    heard as "clean". Neither is counted as wrong, but each would cost a
    pharmacist a call.
  - Answers split in two: a two-part answer like "Yes, correct." split into two
    turns in 20 of 120 calls, so the patient heard the next question again. My
    first draft of the doc said 3 calls; I recounted and corrected it before
    committing.

  Housekeeping:
  - Commit title: it says D29 to D38, but the commit also holds D21 to D28. I
    didn't force-push to rename it.
  - Live server: restarted on port 7860 with the final code. refill.db still has
    its 15 sessions and 6 requests.

  Your decision: when the name is misheard and the date is right, the retry asks
  only for the date again, so the caller ends up locked out. The final run had
  no lockouts, but all 3 in the run spoiled by sleep were this case. I recommend
  asking for name and date again on the retry. That changes one line of your
  CLAUDE.md. Say yes and I'll log it, make the change and retest identity.
  Otherwise it stays in the known limits.

  Still to do after that: a Windows install check, the setup docs and your final
  /export.

※ recap: Final QA on the refill voice agent is done and pushed as c630089: no wrong writes, and the one bug found is fixed. Next you need to decide whether a failed identity retry should ask for both name and date again.

      331  - Cost: "Yes, cancel it" now takes one more turn. A mixed answ

      334 +## D39. Cancelling a refill on a call is checked once, with th
          +e keypad offered (2026-11-07)                                 
      335 +- Options: (A) end the call on the first answer that reads as 
          +a cancel; (B) ask once more by voice; (C) ask once more and of
          +fer keys: "Press 1 to cancel it, or 2 to keep going."         
      336 +- Chosen: C, the engineer's call.                             
      337 +- Why: cancelling is the one answer that ends the call with no
          +thing sent, and D38 showed a recognizer can turn a yes into a 
          +cancel. A key press cannot be misheard. Voice answers still wo
          +rk, because many callers will just say yes or no.             
      338 +- Scope: calls only, after verification. A typed text cannot b
          +e misheard, so SMS cancels at once. Before verification there 
          +is no refill to cancel. Sending already has its own check, the
          + summary with the price.                                      
      339 +- Rules: 1, a plain yes or a cancel word cancels. 2, a plain n
          +o, "keep going" or "send it" keeps going, and at the summary a
          + "send it" sends. Anything else gets the question again. Keys 
          +1 and 2 do nothing at any other time. This replaces D38 on cal
          +ls: a mixed yes and cancel now gets this check instead of the 
          +plain question.                                               
      340 +- Cost: a real cancel on a call takes one more turn.          
      341 +                                                              
      342 +## D40. A shared test link on Railway with a TURN relay (2026-
          +11-07)                                                        
      343 +- Options: (A) a tunnel from my Mac; (B) Railway with a relay;
          + (C) a cloud VM with open UDP ports.                          
      344 +- Chosen: B, the engineer's call. Railway takes no inbound UDP
          +, so call audio goes through Metered's TURN relay (free plan, 
          +500 MB a month, no card). Cloudflare's relay asked for a card.
      345 +- Why: reviewers need a link that works when my Mac is off. A 
          +relay also gets through office networks that block direct audi
          +o.                                                            
      346 +- Shared-link rules: an access code in the link guards every e
          +ndpoint, because the link spends real API credit. Each browser
          + gets its own made-up caller number (555-01xx, a range kept fo
          +r fiction), so testers never see each other's texts. At most t
          +hree calls run at once. The page says calls are recorded and g
          +ives the demo patient's details.                              
      347 +- Cost: audio takes an extra hop through the relay. When the 5
          +00 MB runs out, calls from strict networks fail until the next
          + month.

⏺ Your test link is live, and every feature now has a short guide. On a call,
  cancelling a refill is now checked with the keypad before the call ends.
  Everything is committed and pushed as f39ec5e.

  Link to share: https://refill-line-production.up.railway.app/?code=6f828e8858

  - It only works with the ?code= part, because each call spends real API
    credit.
  - The page shows the demo patient and a recording notice, plus a "How to test
    this line" link to the guide (also in the repo as docs/TESTING_GUIDE.md).
  - Each browser gets its own made-up caller number, so testers never see each
    other's texts.
  - At most three calls can run at once.
  - It stays up whether or not your Mac is on.

  Cancel check (D39): on a call, after verification, an answer that sounds like
  a cancel now gets: "Just to be sure. Do you want to cancel this refill? Press
  1 to cancel it, or 2 to keep going."
  - 1, "yes" or "cancel" cancels the refill.
  - 2, "no" or "keep going" reads the summary again. "No, send it" sends it.
  - Keys 1 and 2 do nothing at any other time.
  - Texts still cancel right away, since a typed message can't be misheard.
  - I only added the check to cancelling. Sending already has its own
    confirmation: the summary with the price.

  Testing:
  - Typed scenarios: 805 of 806 passed, with 0 rule violations. The one miss is
    the store-hours path that was already known to be flaky; it passed 6 of 6 on
    a rerun.
  - Scripted call moves: 27 of 27 passed, including a new keypad cancel-check
    move.
  - Exit checks, hostile input, SMS fallback: all clean.
  - The link itself: three full calls from this Mac completed, two of them
    forced to send audio only through the relay, the way a strict office network
    would.
  - Replies on the link: median 1.12 to 1.42 s, against 0.86 s locally.
  - Older results: the 120-call matrix numbers are from the code before the
    cancel check. No caller in it cancels, so it never reaches the check.

  Accounts I set up with your sanat.offbus Google account:
  - Cloudflare: the login already existed, with a "sanat" and a "[another account]"
    account. Its relay wanted a card, so I subscribed to nothing there.
  - Metered: the relay now in use. It's the free 500 MB-a-month plan with no
    card, enough for a few hundred calls. The 20 GB free plan needs a card for
    verification only. The login password is METERED_ACCOUNT_PASSWORD in .env.
  - Railway: a new project, refill-voice-agent, on your existing account. It
    runs on your plan's usage, with a volume that keeps calls and recordings.

  Local setup: your refill.db is migrated, with all data intact and a backup in
  the scratchpad. The local server is restarted on the new code, and local calls
  still connect directly, without the relay.

  Still your call: when the name is misheard but the date is right, the retry
  asks only for the date again. It's still listed as a known limit.

  Sources:
  - Railway: inbound UDP not supported
  - Cloudflare TURN FAQ and pricing
  - Metered pricing
  - Pipecat SmallWebRTC transport, ICE servers
